People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. I don’t know if there’s “no reason,” exactly. Like, my brain can usually find some tiny reason and then make it feel bigger than it probably is. But I’m not sure I’d say it comes out of nowhere during the day.

Sometimes I’ll be at work and start thinking, did I miss something, or did I send that email right, but then again that’s kind of part of the job because if a number is off by even a little bit, it matters. And then I’ll notice my mug is still in the sink from the morning and somehow that turns into thinking about cleaning, and then I’m thinking about whether I bought paper towels—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.

So… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed random worrying for no reason, more like I get caught on little things.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing is my sleep has gotten really messed up. Like, I’ll be lying there half the night and my mind just won’t shut up, or I’ll wake up way too early and feel like I didn’t sleep at all. It’s been throwing me off… and then I start worrying about work stuff, like invoices I might’ve missed, which probably doesn’t help.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things throughout the day, not just when you're trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I worry about work stuff, yeah, like if I forgot something or if numbers aren’t matching, but that kind of comes with the job, I guess. Sometimes it follows me around during the day, but I don’t know if I’d call it excessive. Like yesterday I spent way too long thinking about one email, and then I ended up reorganizing my desk instead of answering it—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. I guess I just notice it more at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things when you're not trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes? It’s hard to separate, because I’ll be doing something normal, like entering receipts or checking totals, and then my brain sort of latches onto one little thing. But then sometimes I’m just thinking about lunch or whether I need to do laundry, so it’s not like I’m constantly panicking or anything. \n\nI guess I haven’t really tracked it during the day. At night it’s just more obvious because there’s nothing else going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you worry more than your friends or family members about everyday things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. My mom would probably say yes, but she also worries about everything herself, so I don’t know if she’s the best comparison. And my friends don’t really talk about that stuff much—we’ll talk about bills or work or, like, what happened at the grocery store, but not “how much do you worry,” you know?\n\nI guess I notice my own worrying more because I’m stuck in my own head. I don’t know if it’s more than other people or just… louder to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or fatigued during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, actually. Even if I’m in bed all night, I’ll get up and feel like I didn’t really get restored or whatever. Like walking from the parking lot into the office can feel stupidly hard, like I’m dragging sandbags.\n\nAnd then by lunch my brain is just worn out. I’ll be staring at numbers and it’s like they’re all there, but my head is already done. Although sometimes I wonder if it’s just the fluorescent lights in the office—those things are awful, and my coworker keeps this little fan on that makes this clicking sound… sorry. But yeah, tired during the day, pretty often.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things during the day even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s “no reason,” exactly. Like, my brain can usually find some tiny reason and then make it feel bigger than it probably is. But I’m not sure I’d say it comes out of nowhere during the day.\n\nSometimes I’ll be at work and start thinking, did I miss something, or did I send that email right, but then again that’s kind of part of the job because if a number is off by even a little bit, it matters. And then I’ll notice my mug is still in the sink from the morning and somehow that turns into thinking about cleaning, and then I’m thinking about whether I bought paper towels—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed random worrying for no reason, more like I get caught on little things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that aren't related to work or your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um… not really that I can point to. I mean, if it’s not work or bills or chores or some kind of thing I’m supposed to handle, I don’t know that I’m sitting there worrying about, like… random stuff.\n\nSometimes I’ll start thinking about something weird, like whether I was awkward in a conversation from three years ago, but then I’ll remember I need to switch the laundry, and then I’m annoyed because my apartment washers are always full—there’s this one neighbor who leaves their clothes in there forever.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed worrying that isn’t tied to some responsibility or something practical.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. But yeah, tired during the day, pretty often.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things during the day even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s “no reason,” exactly. Like, my brain can usually find some tiny reason and then make it feel bigger than it probably is. But I’m not sure I’d say it comes out of nowhere during the day.\n\nSometimes I’ll be at work and start thinking, did I miss something, or did I send that email right, but then again that’s kind of part of the job because if a number is off by even a little bit, it matters. And then I’ll notice my mug is still in the sink from the morning and somehow that turns into thinking about cleaning, and then I’m thinking about whether I bought paper towels—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed random worrying for no reason, more like I get caught on little things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that aren't related to work or your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um… not really that I can point to. I mean, if it’s not work or bills or chores or some kind of thing I’m supposed to handle, I don’t know that I’m sitting there worrying about, like… random stuff.\n\nSometimes I’ll start thinking about something weird, like whether I was awkward in a conversation from three years ago, but then I’ll remember I need to switch the laundry, and then I’m annoyed because my apartment washers are always full—there’s this one neighbor who leaves their clothes in there forever.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed worrying that isn’t tied to some responsibility or something practical.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. It’s kind of both, actually, which is annoying. At work I’ll catch myself moving really slow, like I’m just staring at the calculator before I even hit the buttons, and I’m thinking, okay, why is this taking me so long?\n\nBut then other times I can’t sit still. I’ll be messing with receipts or straightening papers or tapping my pen, and then I realize I’m not even doing anything useful, I’m just… fidgeting. It depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your relationships with friends and family?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I care about them, obviously. I’ll think about whether I should call my mom back, or if I sounded weird in a text, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly.\n\nWith friends, I’m kind of bad at replying sometimes, and then suddenly it’s been four days and I’m like, okay, now it’s awkward. But then I’ll get distracted because my phone has all these notifications and half of them are just store coupons, which I really need to turn off because they stress me out more than anything.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed myself worrying a lot about relationships, not in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things that have no direct impact on your work, finances, or daily tasks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I don’t usually sit around worrying about things that don’t affect me directly. I mean, unless I see some awful news story or something and then I’ll think about it for a bit, but then I’m also like, wait, did I take the chicken out of the freezer, and then I’m back to that.\n\nSo… no, I guess not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other sleep-related problems, such as difficulty falling asleep, staying asleep, or experiencing restless sleep, beyond just lying awake at night or waking up too early?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the sleep thing is kind of the main reason I came in, honestly. It’s gotten really messed up.\n\nSome nights I’m just up half the night because my mind won’t stop going, like I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I logged something wrong at work, and then I’m remembering I need laundry detergent—sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, I can’t fall asleep.\n\nAnd then other times I do fall asleep, but I wake up way too early and I feel like I didn’t actually rest at all. Like I was technically asleep, but it doesn’t feel like it counted.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any disturbing dreams or nightmares?\n\nPatient: Not really nightmares, I don’t think. I mean, I’ll wake up unsettled sometimes, but it’s more like… the whole sleep situation is just really messed up.\n\nWhat brings me in is I’m either up half the night with my mind running, or I wake up way too early and feel like I didn’t actually rest. Like I slept, but it doesn’t count somehow. The dream part isn’t the main thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a strong urge to move your legs or experienced uncomfortable sensations in them when trying to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a strong urge to move them, no.\n\nSometimes I’m restless in general, like I can’t get comfortable and I’ll flip over a bunch, and then I start thinking about how early I have to get up and that makes it worse. Yeah, I mean… I think stressed is probably the right word. When I can’t fall asleep, my mind just keeps running, and then I start noticing the time and doing the math, like, “Okay, if I fall asleep now I’ll get five hours,” and then it’s four, and that just makes it worse.

Although sometimes I wonder if it’s just the fluorescent lights in the office—those things are awful, and my coworker keeps this little fan on that makes this clicking sound… sorry. But yeah, tired during the day, pretty often.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things during the day even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s “no reason,” exactly. Like, my brain can usually find some tiny reason and then make it feel bigger than it probably is. But I’m not sure I’d say it comes out of nowhere during the day.\n\nSometimes I’ll be at work and start thinking, did I miss something, or did I send that email right, but then again that’s kind of part of the job because if a number is off by even a little bit, it matters. And then I’ll notice my mug is still in the sink from the morning and somehow that turns into thinking about cleaning, and then I’m thinking about whether I bought paper towels—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed random worrying for no reason, more like I get caught on little things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that aren't related to work or your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um… not really that I can point to. I mean, if it’s not work or bills or chores or some kind of thing I’m supposed to handle, I don’t know that I’m sitting there worrying about, like… random stuff.\n\nSometimes I’ll start thinking about something weird, like whether I was awkward in a conversation from three years ago, but then I’ll remember I need to switch the laundry, and then I’m annoyed because my apartment washers are always full—there’s this one neighbor who leaves their clothes in there forever.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed worrying that isn’t tied to some responsibility or something practical.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. It’s kind of both, actually, which is annoying. At work I’ll catch myself moving really slow, like I’m just staring at the calculator before I even hit the buttons, and I’m thinking, okay, why is this taking me so long?\n\nBut then other times I can’t sit still. I’ll be messing with receipts or straightening papers or tapping my pen, and then I realize I’m not even doing anything useful, I’m just… fidgeting. It depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your relationships with friends and family?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I care about them, obviously. I’ll think about whether I should call my mom back, or if I sounded weird in a text, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly.\n\nWith friends, I’m kind of bad at replying sometimes, and then suddenly it’s been four days and I’m like, okay, now it’s awkward. But then I’ll get distracted because my phone has all these notifications and half of them are just store coupons, which I really need to turn off because they stress me out more than anything.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed myself worrying a lot about relationships, not in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things that have no direct impact on your work, finances, or daily tasks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Or at least not often enough that I’d notice it as a thing.\n\nLike, if I’m worrying, it’s usually because there’s something attached to it—rent, work stuff, errands, whether I forgot to pay something. I don’t usually sit around worrying about things that don’t affect me directly. I mean, unless I see some awful news story or something and then I’ll think about it for a bit, but then I’m also like, wait, did I take the chicken out of the freezer, and then I’m back to that.\n\nSo… no, I guess not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other sleep-related problems, such as difficulty falling asleep, staying asleep, or experiencing restless sleep, beyond just lying awake at night or waking up too early?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the sleep thing is kind of the main reason I came in, honestly. It’s gotten really messed up.\n\nSome nights I’m just up half the night because my mind won’t stop going, like I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I logged something wrong at work, and then I’m remembering I need laundry detergent—sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, I can’t fall asleep.\n\nAnd then other times I do fall asleep, but I wake up way too early and I feel like I didn’t actually rest at all. Like I was technically asleep, but it doesn’t feel like it counted.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any disturbing dreams or nightmares?\n\nPatient: Not really nightmares, I don’t think. I mean, I’ll wake up unsettled sometimes, but it’s more like… the whole sleep situation is just really messed up.\n\nWhat brings me in is I’m either up half the night with my mind running, or I wake up way too early and feel like I didn’t actually rest. Like I slept, but it doesn’t count somehow. The dream part isn’t the main thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a strong urge to move your legs or experienced uncomfortable sensations in them when trying to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a strong urge to move them, no.\n\nSometimes I’m restless in general, like I can’t get comfortable and I’ll flip over a bunch, and then I start thinking about how early I have to get up and that makes it worse. But my legs specifically? I haven’t really noticed anything like tingling or that crawling feeling people talk about. I mean, unless sitting funny on the couch counts, but that’s different.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think that’s a big thing for me.\n\nDoctor: Has your sleep problem been affecting your ability to function at work or in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still getting to work, so it’s not like I’m completely falling apart or anything. But it’s definitely affecting me.\n\nI do bookkeeping, so details matter, and I’ve made enough reconciliation mistakes lately that my supervisor actually pulled me aside. That was embarrassing. I kept thinking, like, I know how to do this, why am I missing these things?\n\nAnd outside work I’ve been kind of disappearing. I used to go to salsa class, and I’d meet up with friends, but lately I just avoid it because I don’t feel like myself. I’ll tell myself I’m too tired or I’ll go next week, and then I just don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or stressed when you wake up too early or have trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think stressed is probably the right word. When I can’t fall asleep, my mind just keeps running, and then I start noticing the time and doing the math, like, “Okay, if I fall asleep now I’ll get five hours,” and then it’s four, and that just makes it worse.\n\nAnd when I wake up too early, it’s not like I feel refreshed. It’s more like I open my eyes and already feel like I didn’t actually rest, even if technically I slept. Then I’m just lying there annoyed and tense, thinking the whole day is going to be harder. So yeah, the sleep being so messed up is definitely stressing me out.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’ll tell myself I’m too tired or I’ll go next week, and then I just don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or stressed when you wake up too early or have trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think stressed is probably the right word. When I can’t fall asleep, my mind just keeps running, and then I start noticing the time and doing the math, like, “Okay, if I fall asleep now I’ll get five hours,” and then it’s four, and that just makes it worse.\n\nAnd when I wake up too early, it’s not like I feel refreshed. It’s more like I open my eyes and already feel like I didn’t actually rest, even if technically I slept. Then I’m just lying there annoyed and tense, thinking the whole day is going to be harder. So yeah, the sleep being so messed up is definitely stressing me out.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you usually feel anxious when you're lying awake at night?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 26 / bookkeeping_accounting_or_auditing_clerk / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Sleep_Disturbance\nmanifestation: \"What brings me in is that my sleep has gotten really messed up—I’ll either stay up half the night with my mind running or wake up way too early feeling like I never rested.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. It’s like the second I’m lying there and not falling asleep, my brain starts going. Not even always one clear worry, just… thoughts running, jumping around, and then I notice I’m still awake and that makes me more keyed up.

Honestly, that’s kind of what brought me in. My sleep is just really messed up lately — either I’m up half the night with my mind going, or I wake up way too early and feel like I never actually rested. And then I’m trying to get through the day pretending I’m fine, which… I don’t know, it’s exhausting.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF41.1, F51.0, G47.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing is my sleep has gotten really messed up. Like, I’ll be lying there half the night and my mind just won’t shut up, or I’ll wake up way too early and feel like I didn’t sleep at all. It’s been throwing me off… and then I start worrying about work stuff, like invoices I might’ve missed, which probably doesn’t help.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things throughout the day, not just when you're trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I worry about work stuff, yeah, like if I forgot something or if numbers aren’t matching, but that kind of comes with the job, I guess. Sometimes it follows me around during the day, but I don’t know if I’d call it excessive. Like yesterday I spent way too long thinking about one email, and then I ended up reorganizing my desk instead of answering it—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. I guess I just notice it more at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things when you're not trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes? It’s hard to separate, because I’ll be doing something normal, like entering receipts or checking totals, and then my brain sort of latches onto one little thing. But then sometimes I’m just thinking about lunch or whether I need to do laundry, so it’s not like I’m constantly panicking or anything. \n\nI guess I haven’t really tracked it during the day. At night it’s just more obvious because there’s nothing else going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you worry more than your friends or family members about everyday things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. My mom would probably say yes, but she also worries about everything herself, so I don’t know if she’s the best comparison. And my friends don’t really talk about that stuff much—we’ll talk about bills or work or, like, what happened at the grocery store, but not “how much do you worry,” you know?\n\nI guess I notice my own worrying more because I’m stuck in my own head. I don’t know if it’s more than other people or just… louder to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or fatigued during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, actually. Even if I’m in bed all night, I’ll get up and feel like I didn’t really get restored or whatever. Like walking from the parking lot into the office can feel stupidly hard, like I’m dragging sandbags.\n\nAnd then by lunch my brain is just worn out. I’ll be staring at numbers and it’s like they’re all there, but my head is already done. Although sometimes I wonder if it’s just the fluorescent lights in the office—those things are awful, and my coworker keeps this little fan on that makes this clicking sound… sorry. But yeah, tired during the day, pretty often.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things during the day even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s “no reason,” exactly. Like, my brain can usually find some tiny reason and then make it feel bigger than it probably is. But I’m not sure I’d say it comes out of nowhere during the day.\n\nSometimes I’ll be at work and start thinking, did I miss something, or did I send that email right, but then again that’s kind of part of the job because if a number is off by even a little bit, it matters. And then I’ll notice my mug is still in the sink from the morning and somehow that turns into thinking about cleaning, and then I’m thinking about whether I bought paper towels—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed random worrying for no reason, more like I get caught on little things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that aren't related to work or your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um… not really that I can point to. I mean, if it’s not work or bills or chores or some kind of thing I’m supposed to handle, I don’t know that I’m sitting there worrying about, like… random stuff.\n\nSometimes I’ll start thinking about something weird, like whether I was awkward in a conversation from three years ago, but then I’ll remember I need to switch the laundry, and then I’m annoyed because my apartment washers are always full—there’s this one neighbor who leaves their clothes in there forever.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed worrying that isn’t tied to some responsibility or something practical.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. It’s kind of both, actually, which is annoying. At work I’ll catch myself moving really slow, like I’m just staring at the calculator before I even hit the buttons, and I’m thinking, okay, why is this taking me so long?\n\nBut then other times I can’t sit still. I’ll be messing with receipts or straightening papers or tapping my pen, and then I realize I’m not even doing anything useful, I’m just… fidgeting. It depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your relationships with friends and family?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I care about them, obviously. I’ll think about whether I should call my mom back, or if I sounded weird in a text, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly.\n\nWith friends, I’m kind of bad at replying sometimes, and then suddenly it’s been four days and I’m like, okay, now it’s awkward. But then I’ll get distracted because my phone has all these notifications and half of them are just store coupons, which I really need to turn off because they stress me out more than anything.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed myself worrying a lot about relationships, not in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things that have no direct impact on your work, finances, or daily tasks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Or at least not often enough that I’d notice it as a thing.\n\nLike, if I’m worrying, it’s usually because there’s something attached to it—rent, work stuff, errands, whether I forgot to pay something. I don’t usually sit around worrying about things that don’t affect me directly. I mean, unless I see some awful news story or something and then I’ll think about it for a bit, but then I’m also like, wait, did I take the chicken out of the freezer, and then I’m back to that.\n\nSo… no, I guess not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other sleep-related problems, such as difficulty falling asleep, staying asleep, or experiencing restless sleep, beyond just lying awake at night or waking up too early?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the sleep thing is kind of the main reason I came in, honestly. It’s gotten really messed up.\n\nSome nights I’m just up half the night because my mind won’t stop going, like I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I logged something wrong at work, and then I’m remembering I need laundry detergent—sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, I can’t fall asleep.\n\nAnd then other times I do fall asleep, but I wake up way too early and I feel like I didn’t actually rest at all. Like I was technically asleep, but it doesn’t feel like it counted.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any disturbing dreams or nightmares?\n\nPatient: Not really nightmares, I don’t think. I mean, I’ll wake up unsettled sometimes, but it’s more like… the whole sleep situation is just really messed up.\n\nWhat brings me in is I’m either up half the night with my mind running, or I wake up way too early and feel like I didn’t actually rest. Like I slept, but it doesn’t count somehow. The dream part isn’t the main thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a strong urge to move your legs or experienced uncomfortable sensations in them when trying to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a strong urge to move them, no.\n\nSometimes I’m restless in general, like I can’t get comfortable and I’ll flip over a bunch, and then I start thinking about how early I have to get up and that makes it worse.     "content": "/no_think\nYou are a patient who has come to a psychiatric clinic with Bipolar II Disorder. Yeah… I guess it has, mostly because I’ve kind of been pulling back from people. Like, I used to go to salsa class pretty regularly, and I’d see friends through that, or we’d grab food after, and lately I just keep saying I’m too tired or I’ll go next week.

But then I’ll get distracted because my phone has all these notifications and half of them are just store coupons, which I really need to turn off because they stress me out more than anything.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed myself worrying a lot about relationships, not in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things that have no direct impact on your work, finances, or daily tasks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Or at least not often enough that I’d notice it as a thing.\n\nLike, if I’m worrying, it’s usually because there’s something attached to it—rent, work stuff, errands, whether I forgot to pay something. I mean, if it’s not work or bills or chores or some kind of thing I’m supposed to handle, I don’t know that I’m sitting there worrying about, like… random stuff.\n\nSometimes I’ll start thinking about something weird, like whether I was awkward in a conversation from three years ago, but then I’ll remember I need to switch the laundry, and then I’m annoyed because my apartment washers are always full—there’s this one neighbor who leaves their clothes in there forever.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed worrying that isn’t tied to some responsibility or something practical.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. It’s kind of both, actually, which is annoying. At work I’ll catch myself moving really slow, like I’m just staring at the calculator before I even hit the buttons, and I’m thinking, okay, why is this taking me so long?\n\nBut then other times I can’t sit still. I’ll be messing with receipts or straightening papers or tapping my pen, and then I realize I’m not even doing anything useful, I’m just… fidgeting. It depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your relationships with friends and family?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I care about them, obviously. I’ll think about whether I should call my mom back, or if I sounded weird in a text, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly.\n\nWith friends, I’m kind of bad at replying sometimes, and then suddenly it’s been four days and I’m like, okay, now it’s awkward. But then I’ll get distracted because my phone has all these notifications and half of them are just store coupons, which I really need to turn off because they stress me out more than anything.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed myself worrying a lot about relationships, not in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things that have no direct impact on your work, finances, or daily tasks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Or at least not often enough that I’d notice it as a thing.\n\nLike, if I’m worrying, it’s usually because there’s something attached to it—rent, work stuff, errands, whether I forgot to pay something. I don’t usually sit around worrying about things that don’t affect me directly. I mean, unless I see some awful news story or something and then I’ll think about it for a bit, but then I’m also like, wait, did I take the chicken out of the freezer, and then I’m back to that.\n\nSo… no, I guess not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other sleep-related problems, such as difficulty falling asleep, staying asleep, or experiencing restless sleep, beyond just lying awake at night or waking up too early?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the sleep thing is kind of the main reason I came in, honestly. It’s gotten really messed up.\n\nSome nights I’m just up half the night because my mind won’t stop going, like I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I logged something wrong at work, and then I’m remembering I need laundry detergent—sorry, that’s not really the point. But then I’ll get distracted because my phone has all these notifications and half of them are just store coupons, which I really need to turn off because they stress me out more than anything.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed myself worrying a lot about relationships, not in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things that have no direct impact on your work, finances, or daily tasks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Or at least not often enough that I’d notice it as a thing.\n\nLike, if I’m worrying, it’s usually because there’s something attached to it—rent, work stuff, errands, whether I forgot to pay something. I don’t usually sit around worrying about things that don’t affect me directly. I mean, unless I see some awful news story or something and then I’ll think about it for a bit, but then I’m also like, wait, did I take the chicken out of the freezer, and then I’m back to that.\n\nSo… no, I guess not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other sleep-related problems, such as difficulty falling asleep, staying asleep, or experiencing restless sleep, beyond just lying awake at night or waking up too early?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the sleep thing is kind of the main reason I came in, honestly. It’s gotten really messed up.\n\nSome nights I’m just up half the night because my mind won’t stop going, like I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I logged something wrong at work, and then I’m remembering I need laundry detergent—sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, I can’t fall asleep.\n\nAnd then other times I do fall asleep, but I wake up way too early and I feel like I didn’t actually rest at all. Like I was technically asleep, but it doesn’t feel like it counted.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any disturbing dreams or nightmares?\n\nPatient: Not really nightmares, I don’t think. I mean, I’ll wake up unsettled sometimes, but it’s more like… the whole sleep situation is just really messed up.\n\nWhat brings me in is I’m either up half the night with my mind running, or I wake up way too early and feel like I didn’t actually rest. Like I slept, but it doesn’t count somehow. The dream part isn’t the main thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a strong urge to move your legs or experienced uncomfortable sensations in them when trying to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a strong urge to move them, no.\n\nSometimes I’m restless in general, like I can’t get comfortable and I’ll flip over a bunch, and then I start thinking about how early I have to get up and that makes it worse. I mean, unless sitting funny on the couch counts, but that’s different.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think that’s a big thing for me.\n\nDoctor: Has your sleep problem been affecting your ability to function at work or in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still getting to work, so it’s not like I’m completely falling apart or anything. But it’s definitely affecting me.\n\nI do bookkeeping, so details matter, and I’ve made enough reconciliation mistakes lately that my supervisor actually pulled me aside. That was embarrassing. I kept thinking, like, I know how to do this, why am I missing these things?\n\nAnd outside work I’ve been kind of disappearing. I used to go to salsa class, and I’d meet up with friends, but lately I just avoid it because I don’t feel like myself. I’ll tell myself I’m too tired or I’ll go next week, and then I just don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or stressed when you wake up too early or have trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think stressed is probably the right word. When I can’t fall asleep, my mind just keeps running, and then I start noticing the time and doing the math, like, “Okay, if I fall asleep now I’ll get five hours,” and then it’s four, and that just makes it worse.\n\nAnd when I wake up too early, it’s not like I feel refreshed. Like, I used to go to salsa class pretty regularly, and I’d see friends through that, or we’d grab food after, and lately I just keep saying I’m too tired or I’ll go next week.\n\nAnd then next week comes and I still don’t go. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing is my sleep has gotten really messed up. Like, I’ll be lying there half the night and my mind just won’t shut up, or I’ll wake up way too early and feel like I didn’t sleep at all. It’s been throwing me off… and then I start worrying about work stuff, like invoices I might’ve missed, which probably doesn’t help.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about things throughout the day, not just when you're trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I worry about work stuff, yeah, like if I forgot something or if numbers aren’t matching, but that kind of comes with the job, I guess. Sometimes it follows me around during the day, but I don’t know if I’d call it excessive. Like yesterday I spent way too long thinking about one email, and then I ended up reorganizing my desk instead of answering it—sorry, that’s probably not what you asked. I guess I just notice it more at night.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things when you're not trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes? It’s hard to separate, because I’ll be doing something normal, like entering receipts or checking totals, and then my brain sort of latches onto one little thing. But then sometimes I’m just thinking about lunch or whether I need to do laundry, so it’s not like I’m constantly panicking or anything. \n\nI guess I haven’t really tracked it during the day. At night it’s just more obvious because there’s nothing else going on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you worry more than your friends or family members about everyday things?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. My mom would probably say yes, but she also worries about everything herself, so I don’t know if she’s the best comparison. And my friends don’t really talk about that stuff much—we’ll talk about bills or work or, like, what happened at the grocery store, but not “how much do you worry,” you know?\n\nI guess I notice my own worrying more because I’m stuck in my own head. I don’t know if it’s more than other people or just… louder to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or fatigued during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, actually. Even if I’m in bed all night, I’ll get up and feel like I didn’t really get restored or whatever. Like walking from the parking lot into the office can feel stupidly hard, like I’m dragging sandbags.\n\nAnd then by lunch my brain is just worn out. I’ll be staring at numbers and it’s like they’re all there, but my head is already done. Although sometimes I wonder if it’s just the fluorescent lights in the office—those things are awful, and my coworker keeps this little fan on that makes this clicking sound… sorry. But yeah, tired during the day, pretty often.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about things during the day even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if there’s “no reason,” exactly. Like, my brain can usually find some tiny reason and then make it feel bigger than it probably is. But I’m not sure I’d say it comes out of nowhere during the day.\n\nSometimes I’ll be at work and start thinking, did I miss something, or did I send that email right, but then again that’s kind of part of the job because if a number is off by even a little bit, it matters. And then I’ll notice my mug is still in the sink from the morning and somehow that turns into thinking about cleaning, and then I’m thinking about whether I bought paper towels—sorry, that’s not really what you asked.\n\nSo… I guess I’m not sure. I haven’t really noticed random worrying for no reason, more like I get caught on little things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself worrying about things that aren't related to work or your daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: Um… not really that I can point to. I mean, if it’s not work or bills or chores or some kind of thing I’m supposed to handle, I don’t know that I’m sitting there worrying about, like… random stuff.\n\nSometimes I’ll start thinking about something weird, like whether I was awkward in a conversation from three years ago, but then I’ll remember I need to switch the laundry, and then I’m annoyed because my apartment washers are always full—there’s this one neighbor who leaves their clothes in there forever.\n\nSorry. I guess I haven’t really noticed worrying that isn’t tied to some responsibility or something practical.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or on edge during the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. It’s kind of both, actually, which is annoying. At work I’ll catch myself moving really slow, like I’m just staring at the calculator before I even hit the buttons, and I’m thinking, okay, why is this taking me so long?\n\nBut then other times I can’t sit still. I’ll be messing with receipts or straightening papers or tapping my pen, and then I realize I’m not even doing anything useful, I’m just… fidgeting. It depends on the day, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about your relationships with friends and family?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I care about them, obviously. I’ll think about whether I should call my mom back, or if I sounded weird in a text, but I don’t know if I’d call it worrying exactly.\n\nWith friends, I’m kind of bad at replying sometimes, and then suddenly it’s been four days and I’m like, okay, now it’s awkward. But then I’ll get distracted because my phone has all these notifications and half of them are just store coupons, which I really need to turn off because they stress me out more than anything.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really noticed myself worrying a lot about relationships, not in a big way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often worry about things that have no direct impact on your work, finances, or daily tasks?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? Or at least not often enough that I’d notice it as a thing.\n\nLike, if I’m worrying, it’s usually because there’s something attached to it—rent, work stuff, errands, whether I forgot to pay something. I don’t usually sit around worrying about things that don’t affect me directly. I mean, unless I see some awful news story or something and then I’ll think about it for a bit, but then I’m also like, wait, did I take the chicken out of the freezer, and then I’m back to that.\n\nSo… no, I guess not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other sleep-related problems, such as difficulty falling asleep, staying asleep, or experiencing restless sleep, beyond just lying awake at night or waking up too early?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the sleep thing is kind of the main reason I came in, honestly. It’s gotten really messed up.\n\nSome nights I’m just up half the night because my mind won’t stop going, like I’ll be thinking about one thing and then suddenly I’m thinking about whether I logged something wrong at work, and then I’m remembering I need laundry detergent—sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, I can’t fall asleep.\n\nAnd then other times I do fall asleep, but I wake up way too early and I feel like I didn’t actually rest at all. Like I was technically asleep, but it doesn’t feel like it counted.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any disturbing dreams or nightmares?\n\nPatient: Not really nightmares, I don’t think. I mean, I’ll wake up unsettled sometimes, but it’s more like… the whole sleep situation is just really messed up.\n\nWhat brings me in is I’m either up half the night with my mind running, or I wake up way too early and feel like I didn’t actually rest. Like I slept, but it doesn’t count somehow. The dream part isn’t the main thing, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt a strong urge to move your legs or experienced uncomfortable sensations in them when trying to fall asleep?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a strong urge to move them, no.\n\nSometimes I’m restless in general, like I can’t get comfortable and I’ll flip over a bunch, and then I start thinking about how early I have to get up and that makes it worse. But my legs specifically? I haven’t really noticed anything like tingling or that crawling feeling people talk about. I mean, unless sitting funny on the couch counts, but that’s different.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think that’s a big thing for me.\n\nDoctor: Has your sleep problem been affecting your ability to function at work or in your daily activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m still getting to work, so it’s not like I’m completely falling apart or anything. But it’s definitely affecting me.\n\nI do bookkeeping, so details matter, and I’ve made enough reconciliation mistakes lately that my supervisor actually pulled me aside. That was embarrassing. I kept thinking, like, I know how to do this, why am I missing these things?\n\nAnd outside work I’ve been kind of disappearing. I used to go to salsa class, and I’d meet up with friends, but lately I just avoid it because I don’t feel like myself. I’ll tell myself I’m too tired or I’ll go next week, and then I just don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or stressed when you wake up too early or have trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think stressed is probably the right word. When I can’t fall asleep, my mind just keeps running, and then I start noticing the time and doing the math, like, “Okay, if I fall asleep now I’ll get five hours,” and then it’s four, and that just makes it worse.\n\nAnd when I wake up too early, it’s not like I feel refreshed. It’s more like I open my eyes and already feel like I didn’t actually rest, even if technically I slept. Then I’m just lying there annoyed and tense, thinking the whole day is going to be harder. So yeah, the sleep being so messed up is definitely stressing me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. My sleep is just really messed up lately — either I’m up half the night with my mind going, or I wake up way too early and feel like I never actually rested. And then I’m trying to get through the day pretending I’m fine, which… I don’t know, it’s exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Has your sleep problem affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has, mostly because I’ve kind of been pulling back from people. I kept thinking, like, I know how to do this, why am I missing these things?\n\nAnd outside work I’ve been kind of disappearing. I used to go to salsa class, and I’d meet up with friends, but lately I just avoid it because I don’t feel like myself. I’ll tell myself I’m too tired or I’ll go next week, and then I just don’t.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious or stressed when you wake up too early or have trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I mean… I think stressed is probably the right word. When I can’t fall asleep, my mind just keeps running, and then I start noticing the time and doing the math, like, “Okay, if I fall asleep now I’ll get five hours,” and then it’s four, and that just makes it worse.\n\nAnd when I wake up too early, it’s not like I feel refreshed. It’s more like I open my eyes and already feel like I didn’t actually rest, even if technically I slept. Then I’m just lying there annoyed and tense, thinking the whole day is going to be harder. So yeah, the sleep being so messed up is definitely stressing me out.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel anxious when you're lying awake at night?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. My sleep is just really messed up lately — either I’m up half the night with my mind going, or I wake up way too early and feel like I never actually rested. And then I’m trying to get through the day pretending I’m fine, which… I don’t know, it’s exhausting.\n\nDoctor: Has your sleep problem affected your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has, mostly because I’ve kind of been pulling back from people. Like, I used to go to salsa class pretty regularly, and I’d see friends through that, or we’d grab food after, and lately I just keep saying I’m too tired or I’ll go next week.\n\nAnd then next week comes and I still don’t go. It’s not like anyone did anything wrong, I just don’t feel like myself, and it feels easier to stay home. My family notices I’m quieter too, I think, but I mostly try to act normal.\n\nWork has been affected too — I know that’s not exactly relationships, but I’m still showing up, I’m not missing work, but I’ve made enough reconciliation mistakes that my supervisor pulled me aside. That kind of made me want to hide from everyone even more.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your mood or ability to enjoy activities you used to find pleasurable since your sleep problems started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve noticed that. Now I look at them and feel nothing, like they belong to somebody else. I don’t know. It’s not that I hate those things now, it’s just like the part of me that cared is not really there.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worrying and anxiety are the main reasons you're having trouble sleeping?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe partly, yeah, because when I’m lying there my mind does start running. I’ll stay up half the night with my brain going and going, or I’ll wake up way too early and feel like I never actually rested. And then I start thinking about the next day and how tired I’m going to be, which probably doesn’t help… but it all kind of blurs together.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep problems would improve if your worrying and anxiety were completely gone?\n\nPatient: Maybe… a little?
